S3
New notation for proofs

For two integers
m
and
n
,
m\mod n
denotes the remainder when
m
is divided by
n
. For
m\leq n
,
[m:n]
indicates the set
\{m,m+1,\ldots,n\}
.
We denote by
\mathbf{e}^{(i)}_{k}
the unit vector of length
k
with one in the
i
-th position and zero everywhere else. When
k
is clear from the context, we will denote
\mathbf{e}^{(i)}_{k}
only by
\mathbf{e}^{(i)}
.
\mathbf{0}_{m\times n}
will denote a matrix with
m
rows and
n
columns, whose all elements are zero. For two sets
A
and
B
in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
, we define the Minkowski sum
A+B
as the set
\{x+y:x\in A,y\in B\}
. For two real numbers
x
snd
y
, we denote
\min(x,y)
by
x\wedge y
and
\max(x,y)
by
x\vee y
.

The order statistics of
x
will be denoted by
(\mathbf{x}_{[1]},\mathbf{x}_{[2]},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{[k]})
.
We also introduce some shorthand. For any
t\in[T]
, we will denote the vector
(Q_{t}^{*}(H_{t},i))_{i\in[k_{t}]}
by
Q_{t}^{*}(H_{t})
. As mentioned earlier, the
d^{*}
defined by (
2.3
) need not be unique. For ease of representation, unless otherwise mentioned, we will stick to a particular version of
d^{*}
:

\displaystyle d_{t}^{*}(H_{t})=\max(\argmax_{a_{t}\in[k_{t}]}Q_{t}^{*}(H_{t},a_{t}))\quad\text{ for all }t\in[T].

(S3.1)

S4
Proofs for the convex surrogate examples in Section
3

In the proofs for this section,
\tilde{\phi}
will be used to denote an arbitrary function, whose definition can change from proof to proof.

S4.0.1
Proof of Result
1

Proof of Result
1
.

Note that in this case
V^{\psi}(f)
equals

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[-\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}],j\in[k_{2}]}\frac{\exp\bigg(-\Big(f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})-f_{1i}(H_{1})+f_{2A_{2}}(H_{2})-f_{2j}(H_{2})\Big)\bigg)(Y_{1}+Y_{2})}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}],j\in[k_{2}]}\frac{e^{-\Big(f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})-f_{1i}(H_{1})+f_{2A_{2}}(H_{2})-f_{2j}(H_{2})\Big)}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[-\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\bigg\{\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}],j\in[k_{2}]}\exp\bigg(-\Big(f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})-f_{1i}(H_{1})+f_{2a_{2}}(H_{2})-f_{2j}(H_{2})\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \times\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\bigg\}\bigg]

Therefore, for fixed
H_{2}
, if it exists,
\tilde{f}_{2}(H_{2})
is in

\displaystyle\argmax_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}}\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\frac{\bigg\{\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}],j\in[k_{2}]}-e^{-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})+f_{1i}(H_{1})-\mathbf{y}_{a_{2}}+\mathbf{y}_{j}}\bigg\}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})},

provided the above set is non-empty.
However,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}}\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\frac{\bigg\{\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}],j\in[k_{2}]}-e^{-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})+f_{1i}(H_{1})-\mathbf{y}_{a_{2}}+\mathbf{y}_{j}}\bigg\}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -e^{-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}}\bigg(\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}],j\in[k_{2}]}e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})-\mathbf{y}_{a_{2}}+\mathbf{y}_{j}}\bigg)

which equals

\displaystyle-e^{-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}}\bigg\{\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\frac{e^{-\mathbf{y}_{a_{2}}}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\bigg(\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}e^{\mathbf{y}_{j}}\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]}e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})}\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]}e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}}\bigg\{\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\underbrace{\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}}_{c_{a_{2}}(H_{2})}\bigg(\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}e^{\mathbf{y}_{j}-\mathbf{y}_{a_{2}}}\bigg)\bigg\}.

Lemma
S4.1
implies
\tilde{f}_{2}(H_{2})
uniquely exists and

\argmax(\tilde{f}_{2}(H_{2}))=\argmax_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}].

Here Lemma
S4.1
applies because
\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]>0
by Assumption V, which implies
c_{a_{2}}(H_{2})
’s are positive real numbers.
Since
Q_{2}^{*}(H_{2},A_{2})=\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}]
and
\tilde{d}_{2}=\text{pred}(\tilde{f}_{2})
, it follows that
\tilde{d}_{2}(H_{2})\in\argmax_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}Q_{2}^{*}(H_{2},a_{2})
.

Lemma
S4.1
also implies that
\sup_{f_{2}}V^{\psi}(f)
equals

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]}e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}\Big(\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sqrt{C_{j}(H_{2})}\Big)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]}\frac{e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})}}{e^{f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sqrt{C_{j}(H_{2})}\Big)^{2}\mid H_{1},A_{1}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum\limits\limits_{a_{1}\in[k_{1}]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=j]}\Big)^{2}\mid H_{1},A_{1}=a_{1}\bigg]\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]}\frac{e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})}}{e^{f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}}\bigg].

Therefore, for fixed
H_{1}
,
\tilde{f}_{1}(H_{1})
exists if

\displaystyle\argmin\limits_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}}\sum\limits\limits_{a_{1}\in[k_{1}]}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=j]}\Big)^{2}\mid H_{1},A_{1}=a_{1}\Big]\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]}e^{\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{a_{1}}}

is non-empty and in that case, any member of the above set is a candidate for
\tilde{f}_{1}(H_{1})
.
Note that for any
H_{1}
and
A_{1}=a_{1}
,

\Big(\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=j]}\Big)^{2}>0

because
\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}]>0
due to Assumption V.
Applying Lemma
S4.1
, we can show that
the above set is non-empty and

\argmax(\tilde{f}_{1}(H_{1}))=\argmax_{a_{1}\in[k_{1}]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=j]}\Big)^{2}\mid H_{1},A_{1}=a_{1}\bigg].

The proof follows noting

\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}]=Y_{1}+Q^{*}_{2}(H_{2},A_{2})

and

\tilde{d}_{1}(H_{1})=\text{pred}(f_{1}(H_{1}))
.
∎

Lemma S4.1
.

Suppose

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(\mathbf{y})=\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}c_{i}\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k]}e^{\mathbf{y}_{j}-\mathbf{y}_{i}},\quad\text{ for all }\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}},

where
k\in\mathbb{N}
and the
c_{i}
’s are positive real numbers.
Then
\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\tilde{\phi}(\mathbf{y})=(\sum\limits\limits_{i\in k}\sqrt{c_{i}})^{2}
and
\argmin_{\mathbf{y}}\tilde{\phi}(\mathbf{y})
is a singletone set. Moreover,

\mathbf{y}^{*}=\argmin_{\mathbf{y}}\tilde{\phi}(\mathbf{y})
satisfies
\argmax(\mathbf{y}^{*})=\argmax(\mathbf{c})
.

Proof of Lemma
S4.1
.

\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\tilde{\phi}(\mathbf{y})
is equivalent to the infimum of

\displaystyle g_{1}(\mathbf{x})=\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}c_{i}\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k]}\frac{\mathbf{x}_{j}}{\mathbf{x}_{i}},

where
\mathbf{x}_{i}=\exp^{\mathbf{y}_{i}}>0
for each
i\in[k]
. However, the above is the infimum of

\displaystyle g_{2}(\mathbf{u})=\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}c_{i}/\mathbf{u}_{i}

(S4.1)

where
\mathbf{u}_{i}=\mathbf{x}_{i}/\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k]}\mathbf{x}_{j}
satisfies the constraints
\sum\limits\limits_{i=1}^{k}\mathbf{u}_{i}=1
and
\mathbf{u}_{i}\in(0,1)
. Therefore, we need to
minimize the function
\mathbf{u}\mapsto\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}c_{i}/\mathbf{u}_{i}
over the simplex
\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}:\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{1}_{k}=1,\mathbf{u}_{j}\in(0,1)\text{ for all }j\in[k]\}
. By Lagrange multiplier method, we can show that any critical point of the minimization program

\displaystyle\underset{\displaystyle\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}{\mathrm{min}}\quad\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}c_{i}/\mathbf{u}_{i}\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle

(S4.2)

\displaystyle\mathmakebox[\widthof{$\underset{\displaystyle\phantom{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}}{\mathrm{min}}$}][c]{\mathmakebox[\widthof{$\mathrm{min}$}][l]{\mathrm{\kern 1.00006pts.t.}}}\quad

\displaystyle\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}\mathbf{u}_{i}

\displaystyle=1,\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{i}

\displaystyle\in(0,1)\quad\text{ for all }i\in[k].

satisfies

\mathbf{u}_{i}=\sqrt{c_{i}/c_{\text{sum}}}
where
c_{\text{sum}}\in\mathbb{R}
is such that
\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}\mathbf{u}_{i}=1
holds. This yields
c_{\text{sum}}=(\sum\limits\limits_{i\in k}\sqrt{c_{i}})^{2}
, leading to
\mathbf{u}_{i}=\sqrt{c_{i}}/\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}\sqrt{c_{i}}
. Since
c_{i}>0
for all
i\in[k]
, it follows that
\mathbf{u}_{i}\in(0,1)
for
i\in[k]
. Therefore,
\mathbf{u}_{i}^{*}=\sqrt{c_{i}}/\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}\sqrt{c_{i}}
is also the unique solution to the optimization problem (
S4.2
).
The minimum of (
S4.2
) is therefore
(\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}\sqrt{c_{i}})^{2}
. The
\mathbf{y}^{*}_{i}
corresponding to
\mathbf{u}_{i}=\sqrt{c_{i}}/\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k]}\sqrt{c_{i}}
satisfies
\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}=\log(c_i)/2
. Thus
\mathbf{y}^{*}
is the unique maximizer of
\tilde{\phi}(\mathbf{y})
. The proof is complete noticing
\argmax(\mathbf{y}^{*})=\argmax(c)
.
∎

S4.0.2
Proof of Result
2

Proof of Result
2
.

Note that
\psi(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y};a_{1},a_{2})
equals

\displaystyle-e^{-\mathbf{y}_{a_{2}}}\Big(e^{-\mathbf{x}_{a_{1}}}+\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq a_{1}}e^{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\Big)+\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}e^{\mathbf{y}_{j}}\Big(e^{-\mathbf{x}_{a_{1}}}+\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq a_{1}}e^{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\Big(e^{-\mathbf{x}_{a_{1}}}+\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq a_{1}}e^{\mathbf{x}_{i}}\Big)\Big(e^{-\mathbf{y}_{a_{2}}}+\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}e^{\mathbf{y}_{j}}\Big).

When
\psi
is the loss in (
3.11
),
V^{\psi}(f)
equals

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{-\Big(e^{-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}+\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})}\Big)\Big(e^{-f_{2A_{2}}(H_{2})}+\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq A_{2}}e^{f_{2j}(H_{2})}\Big)(Y_{1}+Y_{2})}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[-\Big(e^{-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}+\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \times\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\Big(e^{-f_{2a_{2}}(H_{2})}+\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}e^{f_{2j}(H_{2})}\Big)\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\bigg]

Note that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{2}}V^{\psi}(f_{1},f_{2})=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(e^{-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}+\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \times\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}}\bigg\{-\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\Big(e^{-\mathbf{y}_{a_{2}}}+\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}e^{\mathbf{y}_{j}}\Big)\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},a_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(a_{1}\mid h_{1})}\bigg\}\bigg]

(S4.3)

Therefore, for fixed

for all

.
For the rest of the proof, we will denote terms such as

and

for each

. Hence, the vector
\mathbf{p}(h_{2})\in\mathcal{S}^{k_{2}-1}
.
Fact
S4.1
implies that
\argmax(\tilde{f}_{2}(h_{2}))\subset\argmax(\mathbf{p}(h_{2}))=\argmax_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},a_{2}]
. Therefore,

. Also, Fact
S4.1
implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}}\bigg\{-\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\Big(e^{-\mathbf{y}_{a_{2}}}+\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}e^{\mathbf{y}_{j}}\Big)\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},a_{2}]}{\pi_{1}(a_{1}\mid h_{1})}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\frac{\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},j]}{\pi_{1}(a_{1}\mid h_{1})}\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sqrt{\mathbf{p}(H_{2})_{j}(1-\mathbf{p}(H_{2})_{j})}

\displaystyle=

Therefore, (
S4.3
) implies that
\sup_{f_{2}}V^{\psi}(f)
equals

\displaystyle 2

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[-\Big(e^{-f_{1A_{1}}(H_{1})}+\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \times\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\frac{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]\sum\limits\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},j]}}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\bigg].

Denoting conditional expectation of a random variable
U
with respect to

and

as

equals

\displaystyle\ 2\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum\limits\limits_{a_{1}\in[k_{1}]}\Big(e^{-f_{1a_{1}}(H_{1})}+\sum\limits\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq a_{1}}e^{f_{1i}(H_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \times\sum\limits\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},j]}\bigg|H_{1},a_{1}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle+

where for any random variable
X
and
i\in[k_{1}]
, we use the shorthand

for

.
We overload notation and for all

and

and

.

for all

and

by our assumption.

Another application of Fact

The proof follows noting

Fact S4.1
.

For any
\mathbf{p}\in\mathcal{S}^{k-1}
optimization problem

\displaystyle\underset{\displaystyle\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}{\mathrm{min}}\quad\sum\limits_{j\in[k]}\Big(e^{-\mathbf{y}_{j}}\mathbf{p}_{j}+(1-\mathbf{p}_{j})e^{\mathbf{y}_{j}}\Big)\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle

(S4.4)

has a unique minimizer
\mathbf{y}^{*}
satisfying
\argmax(\mathbf{y}^{*})\subset\argmax(\mathbf{p})
and the minimum value is
2\sum\limits_{j\in[k]}\sqrt{\mathbf{p}_{j}(1-\mathbf{p}_{j})}
.

Proof of Fact
S4.1
.

The given optimization problem is equivalent to

\displaystyle\underset{\displaystyle\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}_{>0}}{\mathrm{min}}\quad\sum\limits_{j\in[k]}\Big(\mathbf{p}_{j}/\mathbf{x}_{j}+(1-\mathbf{p}_{j})\mathbf{x}_{j}\Big)

where we used the variable transformation
\mathbf{x}_{j}=e^{\mathbf{y}_{j}}
for
j\in[k]
. The function
\tilde{\phi}(\mathbf{x})=\sum\limits_{j\in[k]}\Big(\mathbf{p}_{j}/\mathbf{x}_{j}+(1-\mathbf{p}_{j})\mathbf{x}_{j}\Big)
is convex on
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{>0}
. Hence, any critical point
\mathbf{x}^{*}
of
\tilde{\phi}
is the unique global minimum over
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{>0}
. Since
\tilde{\phi}
is differentiable on
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{>0}
, it follows that
\mathbf{x}^{*}
satisfies
\gradient\tilde{\phi}(\mathbf{x}^{*})=0
. Therefore,
\mathbf{x}^{*}_{j}=\sqrt{\mathbf{p}_{j}/(1-\mathbf{p}_{j})}
for all
j\in[k]
, which implies
\tilde{\phi}(\mathbf{x}^{*})=2\sum\limits_{j\in[k]}\sqrt{\mathbf{p}_{j}(1-\mathbf{p}_{j})}
.
Note that the optimal solution of (
S4.4
) satisfies
\mathbf{y}^{*}_{j}=\log(\mx^*_j)
. Therefore,
\mathbf{y}^{*}
is unique and
\mathbf{y}^{*}_{j}=\frac{1}{2}\log(\mp_j/(1-\mp_j))
. Since
\phi(x)=\exp(-x)
is convex, bounded below, differentiable, and decreasing, Theorem 9 of
Zhang (2004)
implies that
\argmax(\mathbf{y}^{*})\subset\argmax(\mathbf{p})
.

∎

S4.0.3
Proof of Result

Proof of Result
3
.

V^{\psi}(f)
equals

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{(Y_{1}+Y_{2})\phi^{(1)}(f_{1}(H_{1});A_{1})}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}+\frac{(Y_{1}+Y_{2})\phi^{(2)}(f_{1}(H_{1});A_{2})}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\bigg]

Since the propensity scores are positive by Assumption I,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\bigg[\phi^{(1)}(f_{1}(H_{1});A_{1})\frac{Y_{1}+Y_{2}}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\bigg]\\
&\ =\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum\limits_{a_{1}\in[k_{1}]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{Y_{1}+Y_{2}}{\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\bigg|H_{1}=h_{1},A_{1}=a_{1}\bigg]\phi^{(1)}(f_{1}(H_{1});a_{1})\bigg]\end{split}

Thus for any

which is positive because of Assumptions V.
In that case,

then

. Therefore, we have shown that
whenever
\tilde{f}_{1}(h_{1})
exists,

\displaystyle\text{pred}(\tilde{f}_{1}(h_{1}))\in\argmax(\mathbf{p}^{(1)})=\argmax_{a_{1}\in[k_{1}]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{(Y_{1}+Y_{2})}{\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\bigg|H_{1}=h_{1},A_{1}=a_{1}\bigg].

Similarly, since
\phi^{(2)}
is also single-stage Fisher consistent, taking
k_{1}
to be
k_{2}
in Lemma
S4.2
, we can show that for a fixed
h_{2}=(h_{1},a_{1},y_{1},o_{2})
, whenever
\tilde{f}_{2}(h_{2})
exists,

\displaystyle\text{pred}(\tilde{f}_{2}(h_{2}))\in

\displaystyle\ \argmax_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{(Y_{1}+Y_{2})}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\bigg|H_{2}=h_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \argmax_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(a_{1}\mid h_{1})}\Big(y_{1}+\mathbb{E}[Y_{2}\mid H_{2}=h_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \argmax_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{2}\mid H_{2}=h_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}],

where we used the fact that
\pi_{1}(a_{1}\mid h_{1})>0
(by Assumption I). The proof follows noting
\tilde{d}_{t}(H_{t})=\text{pred}(f_{t}(H_{t}))
for
t=1,2
.
∎

Lemma S4.2
.

Suppose
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is a Fisher consistent surrogate for
T=1
. Then if
\argmax_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}}\Psi(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p})
is non-empty for some
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}_{\geq 0}
with
\Psi
as defined in (
4.2
), then for any
\mathbf{x}^{*}\in\argmax_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}}\Psi(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p})
, it follows that
\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{*})}=\max(\mathbf{p})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}_{>0}
. Consider the distribution
\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{P}^{0}
so that
H_{1}=\emptyset
and
\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}\mid A_{1}=i]=\mathbf{p}_{i}
for all
i\in[k_{1}]
. For such
\mathbb{P}
,
f_{1}:\mathcal{H}_{1}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}
is just a vector in
\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}
. Since
\mathbb{P}
also satisfies Assumption I-III, for any such
f_{1}
, it holds that

\displaystyle V(f_{1})=\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}1[\text{pred}(f_{1})=A_{1}]/\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})]=\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(f_{1})},

and

\displaystyle V^{\phi}(f_{1})=\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}\phi(f_{1},A_{1})/\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})]=\Psi(f_{1};\mathbf{p}).

Let
\tilde{f}_{1}
be the maximizer of
V^{\phi}(f_{1})
over
f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}
.
Therefore, if
\argmax_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}}\Psi(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p})
is non-empty, then
\tilde{f}_{1}
exists. In that case, any
\mathbf{x}^{*}\in\argmax_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}}\Psi(f_{1};\mathbf{p})
is a candidate of
\tilde{f}_{1}
. Since
\tilde{f}_{1}
exists and
\phi
is Fisher consistent for
T=1
, Definition
2.1
(definition of Fisher consistency) implies
V(\tilde{f}_{1})=\sup_{f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}}V(f_{1})
. Therefore, it follows that
\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{*})}=\max(\mathbf{p})
for any
\mathbf{x}^{*}\in\argmax_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}}\Psi(f_{1};\mathbf{p})
.
∎

S4.0.4
Proofs for Example
6

First, we derive a formula for
\tilde{f}_{1}(h_{1})
under the general two-stage setup. It will be used in Section
S4.0.4
, where we derive a simpler form of
\tilde{f}_{1}(h_{1})
under the toy setup.
In this case, for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1}}
,
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}
,
a_{1}\in[k_{1}]
, and
a_{2}\in[k_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\psi(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y};a_{1},a_{2})=

\displaystyle\ \sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}\min(-\mathbf{x}_{i}-1,-\mathbf{y}_{j}-1,0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}\max(1+\mathbf{x}_{i},1+\mathbf{y}_{j},0).

(S4.5)

For any set
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathcal{F}_{1}\times\mathcal{F}_{2}
,
\sup_{(f_{1},f_{2})\in\mathcal{C}}V^{\psi}(f_{1},f_{2})
takes the form

\displaystyle\sup_{(f_{1},f_{2})\in\mathcal{C}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(Y_{1}+Y_{2})\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq A_{2}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+f_{2j}(H_{2}),0)}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{(f_{1},f_{2})\in\mathcal{C}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(Y_{1}+Y_{2})\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq A_{2}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+f_{2j}(H_{2}),0)}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\inf_{(f_{1},f_{2})\in\mathcal{C}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(Y_{1}+Y_{2})\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq A_{2}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+f_{2j}(H_{2}),0)}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\right].

Therefore, it suffices to consider the minimization problem.
Let us denote the scores corresponding to
f_{1}
as
f_{1j}
’s for
j\in[k_{1}]
so that
f_{1}=(f_{11},\ldots,f_{1k_{1}})
. Similarly, let us denote the scores corresponding to
f_{2}
as
f_{2j}
’s for
j\in[k_{2}]
so that
f_{2}=(f_{21},\ldots,f_{2k_{2}})
.
Let us denote

\mathcal{R}(f_{1},f_{2})=\mathbb{E}\bigg[(Y_{1}+Y_{2})\frac{\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq A_{2}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+f_{2j}(H_{2}),0)}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\bigg],

which equals

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}]}{\pi(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\frac{\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq A_{2}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+f_{2j}(H_{2}),0)}{\pi_{2}(A_{2}\mid H_{2})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]}\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]:j\neq a_{2}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+f_{2j}(H_{2}),0)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+f_{2j}(H_{2}),0)\sum\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]:a_{2}\neq j}\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\bigg].

Then
\inf_{f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}:\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}f_{2j}=0}\mathcal{R}(f_{1},f_{2})
equals

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}:\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{y}_{j}=0}\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+\mathbf{y}_{j},0)

\displaystyle\ \times\sum\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]:a_{2}\neq j}\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\pi(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\bigg]

For
h_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{2}
, let us denote

\mathbf{p}_{a_{2}}(h_{2})=\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]}{\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},A_{2}=j]}\quad\text{ for all }a_{2}\in[k_{2}]\text{ and }\mathbf{p}(h_{2})=(\mathbf{p}_{a_{2}}(h_{2}))_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]},

which are well-defined since
\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},A_{2}=j]>0
for all

and

due to Assumption V.
Then

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}:\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{y}_{j}=0}\bigg\{\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+\mathbf{y}_{j},0)

\displaystyle\ \times\sum\limits_{a_{2}\in[k_{2}]:a_{2}\neq j}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=a_{2}]\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \bigg(\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},A_{2}=j]\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \times\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}:\sum_{j=1}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{y}_{j}=0}\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+\mathbf{y}_{j},0)\Big(1-p_{j}(H_{2})\Big).

For fixed
H_{1}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\Big(1-p_{j}(H_{2})\Big)\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\max(1+f_{1i}(H_{1}),1+\mathbf{y}_{j},0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ k_{1}\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\Big(1-p_{j}(H_{2})\Big)+\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\Big(1-p_{j}(H_{2})\Big)\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\max(f_{1i}(H_{1}),\mathbf{y}_{j},-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ k_{1}\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\Big(1-p_{j}(H_{2})\Big)+\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\Big(1-p_{j}(H_{2})\Big)\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}-1]}\max(\mathbf{v}_{i}(H_{1};A_{1}),\mathbf{y}_{j}),

where
\mathbf{v}(H_{1};A_{1})=(\max(f_{1i}(H_{1}),-1))_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}
is a vector of length

.
When

and

, Lemma

implies that

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{2}}:\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbf{y}_{j}=0}\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\Big(1-p_{j}(H_{2})\Big)\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}-1]}\max(\mathbf{v}_{i}(H_{1};A_{1}),\mathbf{y}_{j})

\displaystyle\ \begin{cases}\mathbf{v}_{1}(H_{1};A_{1})+\mathbf{v}_{2}(H_{1};A_{1})&\ \text{ if }\min(\mathbf{v}(H_{1};A_{1}))\geq 0\\
\upkappa(\mathbf{v}(H_{1};A_{1}),\mathbf{p}(H_{2}))&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=

where the last step follows because

, in which case,

.

, taking the infimum of

over

\displaystyle\ +\bigg(\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},A_{2}=j]\bigg)\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}f_{1i}(H_{1})1\Big[\min\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}f_{1i}(H_{1})\geq 0\Big]

\displaystyle\ +\bigg(\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid h_{2},A_{2}=j]\bigg)\upkappa(\mathbf{v}(H_{1};A_{1}),\mathbf{p}(H_{2}))1\Big[\min\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}f_{1i}(H_{1})<0\Big].

Therefore, maximizing

with respect to

is equivalent to minimizing

\displaystyle\ +\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=j]\upkappa(\mathbf{v}(H_{1};A_{1}),\mathbf{p}(H_{2}))1\Big[\min\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}f_{1i}(H_{1})<0\Big]\bigg)}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\right]

with respect to

. However, the last display equals

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\left[\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})^{-1}\begin{Bmatrix}\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=j]\mid H_{1},A_{1}\Big]&\\
\times\bigg(\sum\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}f_{1i}(H_{1})1\Big[\min\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}f_{1i}(H_{1})\geq 0\Big]&\\

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ +\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{a_{1}\in[k_{1}]}\Big(\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}[Y_{1}+Y_{2}\mid H_{2},A_{2}=j]\mid H_{1},a_{1}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\ \times\upkappa(\mathbf{v}(H_{1};A_{1}),\mathbf{p}(H_{2}))1\Big[\min\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}f_{1i}(H_{1})<0\Big]\bigg].

Let us denote

Then

(S4.6)

subject to

,

.

Toy setup of the sum-zero loss example

Under the toy setup of Example
6
,
H_{1}=\emptyset
. Therefore,
f_{1}(H_{1})\equiv f_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and

\upalpha(H_{1},a_{1})\equiv\upalpha(a_{1})=\sum\limits_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{2}\mid A_{1}=a_{1},A_{2}=j].

Moreover, since
H_{2}\equiv A_{1}
,
\mathbf{p}(H_{2})=\mathbf{p}(A_{1})
, and

\mathbf{p}_{j}(A_{1})=\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{2}\mid A_{1},A_{2}=j]}{\sum_{j\in[k_{2}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{2}\mid A_{1},A_{2}=j]}\text{ for }j\in[k_{2}].

In our example,
H_{1}=\emptyset
, which implies
f_{1}(H_{1})=f_{1}
. Also,
k_{1}=3
.
In this case, (
S4.6
) reduces to the following optimization problem:

\displaystyle\underset{\displaystyle\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\sum_{i\in[k_{1}]}\mathbf{x}_{i}=0}{\mathrm{min}}\quad\sum_{a_{1}\in[k_{1}]}\upalpha(a_{1})\left(\begin{multlined}\sum_{j\in[k_{1}]:j\neq a_{1}}\mathbf{x}_{j}1\Big[\min\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\mathbf{x}_{i}\geq 0\Big]\\
\\
+\upkappa(\mathbf{v}(a_{1}),\mathbf{p}(a_{1}))1\Big[\min\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq a_{1}}\mathbf{x}_{i}<0\Big]\end{multlined}\right)\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle

(S4.7)

\displaystyle\mathmakebox[\widthof{$\underset{\displaystyle\phantom{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\sum_{i\in[k_{1}]}\mathbf{x}_{i}=0}}{\mathrm{min}}$}][c]{\mathmakebox[\widthof{$\mathrm{min}$}][l]{\mathrm{\kern 1.00006pts.t.}}}\quad

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{1}+\mathbf{x}_{2}+\mathbf{x}_{3}

\displaystyle=0,\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}(a_{1})

\displaystyle=(\max(\mathbf{x}_{i},-1))_{i\in[3]:i\neq a_{1}}

Any solution to solution to (
S4.7
) is a candidate of
\tilde{f}_{1}
.
Let us define

\uppsi(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2})=\sum_{a_{1}\in[k_{1}]}\upalpha(a_{1})\left(\begin{multlined}\sum_{j\in[k_{1}]:j\neq a_{1}}\mathbf{x}_{j}1\Big[\min\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq A_{1}}\mathbf{x}_{i}\geq 0\Big]\\
\\
+\upkappa(\mathbf{v}(a_{1}),\mathbf{p}(a_{1}))1\Big[\min\limits_{i\in[k_{1}]:i\neq a_{1}}\mathbf{x}_{i}<0\Big]\end{multlined}\right)

(S4.8)

.

\tilde{f}
is a solution to (

) if and only if
(\tilde{f}_{1},\tilde{f}_{2})
is a solution to

\displaystyle\underset{\displaystyle(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}{\mathrm{min}}\quad\uppsi(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2})\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle\hfil\hfil\displaystyle\displaystyle

(S4.9)

and
\tilde{f}_{3}=-(\tilde{f}_{1}+\tilde{f}_{2})
. The optimization program (

) is an unconstrained optimization problem with 2 variables.
We obtained the surface plot of
\uppsi
using the software R in the three settings mentioned in Table
1
to find out the optimal solution. Figure

gives the surface plots for settings 2 and 3. The plot for setting 1 is skipped because it is similar to that of Setting 2. Finally, in this case,

and

which equals

.
Hence, any member of

is a candidate for

, the first stage optimal treatment assignment. This explains the

.
