\displaystyle\ \chi_{\phi_{1+t}}\sum_{i=1}^{k_{t}}\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};i)\mathbb{E}[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=i]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \chi_{\phi_{1+t}}\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))\mathbb{E}[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \Big(\min_{1+t\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}\Big)\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))

(S6.19)

\displaystyle\ \times\mathbb{E}\Big[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})\Big].

Since

\displaystyle\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t})_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{2+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{2+t}^{*}(H_{2+t}))\mid H_{1+t},A_{1+t}=i]

(S6.20)

for all
i\in[k_{1+t}]
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\ \bigg|\ H_{1+t}\bigg]\ \bigg|\ H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))]-1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]}{\pi_{1+t}(A_{1+t}\mid H_{1+t})}

\displaystyle\ \times\Psi_{2+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{2+t}^{*}(H_{2+t}))\ \bigg|\ H_{1+t}\bigg]\ \bigg|\ H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})\bigg]

(S6.21)

where (a) uses (
S6.9
) on
H_{1+t}
with
i=\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))
and
\mathbf{p}(H_{1+t})=\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t})
, whose expression is given by (
S6.20
). Also, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))]-1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]}{\pi_{1+t}(A_{1+t}\mid H_{1+t})}\Psi_{2+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{2+t}^{*}(H_{2+t}))\ \bigg|\ H_{1+t}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\bigg|H_{1+t}\bigg]

(S6.22)

is non-negative because
\max(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))\geq\kappa(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))
. Since
C_{\phi_{t}}=\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})
and
C_{\phi_{t}}=1
for all
t\in[2:T]
, it follows that
\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))\leq 1
for each
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
for all
t\in[2:T]
. In particular

0\leq\prod_{r=t+1}^{T-1}\phi_{r}\Big(f_{r}(H_{r});\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))\Big)\leq 1\text{ for all }t\geq 1.

Therefore, denoting

\displaystyle S_{11}=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{r=t+1}^{T-1}\phi_{r}\Big(f_{r}(H_{r});\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))\Big)

\displaystyle\ \times\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))]-1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]}{\pi_{1+t}(A_{1+t}\mid H_{1+t})}

\displaystyle\ \times\Psi_{2+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{2+t}^{*}(H_{2+t}))\ \bigg|\ H_{1+t}\bigg]\ \bigg|\ H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})\bigg],

(S6.23)

and using (
S6.21
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})]\geq S_{11}.

(S6.24)

Since
0\leq\prod_{r=t+1}^{T-1}\phi_{r}\Big(f_{r}(H_{r});\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))\Big)
, from (
S6.22
) it follows that
S_{11}\geq 0
.
Also, Lemma
S6.11
implies

\Psi_{2+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{2+t}^{*}(H_{2+t}))=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\prod_{j=t+2}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\ \bigg|\ H_{2+t}\bigg],

where
H_{2+t}
is non-empty because the case under consideration has
t\in[T-2]
. Therefore,

\displaystyle S_{11}=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{r=t+1}^{T-1}\phi_{r}\Big(f_{r}(H_{r});\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))\Big)

\displaystyle\ \times\frac{1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))]-1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]}{\pi_{1+t}(A_{1+t}\mid H_{1+t})}

\displaystyle\ \times\ \Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=t+2}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\bigg|\ H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})\bigg].

An application of (
S6.5
) yields that

\displaystyle S_{11}=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{r=t+1}^{T-1}\phi_{r}\Big(f_{r}(H_{r});\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))\Big)

\displaystyle\ \times\frac{1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))]-1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]}{\pi_{1+t}(A_{1+t}\mid H_{1+t})}

\displaystyle\ \times\ \Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=t+2}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg|\ H_{t}\bigg].

Combining the above with (
S6.19
) and (
S6.24
), and using the fact that
\phi_{t}
’s and
\chi_{\phi_{t}}
’s are non-negative, we obtain the following lower bound for
S_{1}:

\displaystyle S_{1}\geq

\displaystyle\ \Big(\min_{1+t\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}\Big)\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))S_{11}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \Big(\min_{1+t\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}\Big)\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{r=t+1}^{T-1}\phi_{r}\Big(f_{r}(H_{r});\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))\Big)

(S6.25)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\ \frac{\prod_{j=t+2}^{T}1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\prod_{j=t}^{T}\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\ \Big(1[A_{1+t}=d_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t})]-1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]\Big)1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})]\ \bigg|\ H_{t}\bigg]

Since we already argued that
S_{11}\geq 0
, the RHS of (
S6.25
) is non-negative.
Now we will bound
S_{2}
.
By the induction hypothesis, each element of the sum
S_{2}
is non-negative. Therefore,
S_{2}
is bounded below by
\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))
times

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})]\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\Big)\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\ \bigg|\ H_{t}\bigg]

where the last step follows from (
S6.5
).
Since the Induction hypothesis holds for
1+t
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \Big(\min_{2+t\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}\Big)\phi_{1+t}\Big(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\Big)

\displaystyle\ \times\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{r=t+2}^{T-1}\phi_{r}\Big(f_{r}(H_{r}));\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))\Big)\bigg)

(S6.26)

\displaystyle\ \times\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\times 1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t})]

(S6.27)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\ \frac{\prod_{r=t+2}^{T}1[A_{r}=d_{r}^{*}(H_{r})]-\prod_{r=t+2}^{T}1[A_{r}=\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r})]}{\prod_{r=t+1}^{T}\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\ \bigg|\ H_{1+t}\bigg],

which is also non-negative by the induction hypothesis.
Since the
\chi_{\phi_{t}}
’s and the
\phi_{t}
’s are non-negative, we obtain that

\displaystyle S_{2}\geq

\displaystyle\ \Big(\min_{2+t\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}\Big)\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{r=t+1}^{T-1}\phi_{r}\Big(f_{r}(H_{r}));\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \times\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\times 1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t})]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\ \frac{\prod_{r=t+2}^{T}1[A_{r}=d_{r}^{*}(H_{r})]-\prod_{r=t+2}^{T}1[A_{r}=\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))]}{\prod_{r=t+1}^{T}\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\ \bigg|\ H_{t}\bigg],

which is also non-negative.
Adding the above inequality with the inequality in (
S6.25
), we obtain that

\displaystyle S_{1}+S_{2}\geq\Big(\min_{1+t\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}\Big)\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{\text{diff,t}}\mid\ H_{t}\right]

Since we have shown that
S_{11}
(the RHS of (
S6.25
)) and the RHS of (
S6.26
) are non-negative, it also follows that
\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{\text{diff,t}}\mid\ H_{t}\right]
is non-negative.
The induction hypothesis follows for
t
noting (
S6.17
) and (
S6.18
) imply

\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p}_{t}^{*}(H_{t}))-\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p}_{t}(H_{t}))=S_{1}+S_{2},

thus completing the proof of Lemma
S6.15
.
∎

Lemma S6.16
.

Suppose
\mathbb{P}
,
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{T}
, and
Z_{\text{diff,t}}
are as in Lemma
S6.15
. Additionally,
\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))\geq\mathcal{J}_{t}
for some
\mathcal{J}_{t}\geq 0
for all
t\in[T]
and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
. Then
for
t=1,\ldots,T
, for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
, and for any
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
,

\displaystyle\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p}_{t}^{*}(H_{t}))-\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p}_{t}(H_{t}))\geq\Big(\prod_{i=t}^{T}\mathcal{J}_{i}\Big)\Big(\min_{1+t\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}\Big)\mathbb{E}[Z_{\text{diff,t}}\mid H_{t}],

and
\mathbb{E}[Z_{\text{diff,t}}\mid H_{t}]\geq 0
. Here the
\chi_{\phi_{t}}
’s are as in Lemma
S6.8
and
p_{t}(H_{t})\equiv p_{t}(H_{t};f)
.

Proof.

Note that Lemma
S6.15
implies
\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t}),\text{pred}(f_{t}(H_{t})))\geq\mathcal{J}_{t}
since
f_{t}(H_{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
for all
t\in[T]
. If we redo all steps of Lemma
S6.15
replacing

\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r}),\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r})))
and
\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x},\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))
with
\mathcal{J}_{t}
in all lower bounds, Lemma
S6.16
will follow. Since the proof is similar, it has been skipped.
∎

S6.3
Proof of Theorem
4.1

We need to show that if
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V^{\psi}_{*}
for some
\{f^{(m)}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\mathcal{F}
, then
V(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V_{*}
.
Without loss of generality, let us assume
C_{\phi_{t}}=1
for
t\in[2:T]
because we can substitute
\phi_{t}
by
\phi_{t}/C_{\phi_{t}}
otherwise.
We claim that the proof of Fisher consistency follows directly from Lemma
S6.17
below.

Lemma S6.17
.

Suppose
\phi_{1}
satisfies Condition
N1
and
\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{T}
satisfy both Conditions
N1
and
N2
with
C_{\phi_{t}}=1
for
t\in[2;T]
. If
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V^{*}
, then under Assumptions I-V,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\prod_{j=1+t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}

\displaystyle\ \times\frac{\Big(1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}))]-1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]\Big)}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]\to_{m}0

(S6.28)

for all
t\in[T]
, where the products with empty range are taken to be one.

To see how the proof follows the Lemma
S6.17
, first note that

V_{*}-V(f)=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\frac{\prod_{t=1}^{T}1[A_{t}=d_{t}^{*}(H_{t})]-\prod_{t=1}^{T}1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]}{\prod_{t=1}^{T}\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg].

Letting products over empty ranges be one, we calculate

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigg\{\prod_{j=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\prod_{j=1+t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}

\displaystyle\ \times\frac{\Big(1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}))]-1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]\Big)}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigg\{\prod_{j=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\prod_{j=1+t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}

\displaystyle\ \times\frac{\Big(1[A_{t}=d_{t}^{*}(H_{t})]-1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]\Big)}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{t=1}^{T}\prod_{j=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\prod_{j=1+t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\frac{1[A_{t}=d_{t}^{*}(H_{t})]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}

\displaystyle\ -\sum_{t=1}^{T}\prod_{j=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\prod_{j=1+t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{t=1}^{T}\prod_{j=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\prod_{j=t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}

\displaystyle\ -\sum_{t=1}^{T}\prod_{j=1}^{t}\frac{1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\prod_{j=1+t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})},

where (a) follows from Lemmas
S6.11
and
S6.13
. The telescopic sum in the RHS of the above display equals

\frac{\prod_{t=1}^{T}1[A_{t}=d_{t}^{*}(H_{t})]-\prod_{t=1}^{T}1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]}{\prod_{t=1}^{T}\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}.

Thus, we derived that
V_{*}-V(f)
equals

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigg\{\prod_{j=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\prod_{j=1+t}^{T}\\
&\ \times\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\frac{\Big(1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}))]-1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]\Big)}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg\}\bigg],\end{split}

which goes to zero as
m\to\infty
by Lemma
S6.17
. The rest of the proof is devoted towards proving Lemma
S6.17
.

S6.3.1
Proof of Lemma
S6.17

Let
H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})
be the counterfactual
H_{t}
under the DTR
d^{{(m)}}_{t}(H_{t})=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))
. In other word, the random variable
H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})
is distributed as the
H_{t}
in the world where the treatments are assigned according to
d^{{(m)}}
instead of the propensity scores
\pi_{t}
. Thus

H_{1}(d^{{(m)}})\equiv H_{1},\quad H_{2}(d^{{(m)}})=(H_{1},d^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}),O_{2}(d^{{(m)}}_{1}(H_{1})),Y_{1}(d^{{(m)}}_{1}(H_{1}))),

and so on. Thus
H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})
’s distribution is not same as the distribution of
H_{t}
under
\mathbb{P}
for
t\geq 2
.
From Lemma 1 of
Orellana et al. (2010)

(
Murphy et al., 2001b
, see also)
, it follows that for
t\in[T-1]
, the distribution of
H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}})
is absolutely continuous with respect to the distribution of
H_{1+t}
under Assumptions I-III with Radon-Nikodym derivative

\prod_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1[A_{i}=d^{{(m)}}(H_{i})]}{\pi_{i}(A_{i}\mid H_{i})},

which is well defined as
\pi_{i}(A_{i}\mid H_{i})>0
for all
i\in[T]
by Assumption I.
Therefore, under Assumptions I-III, for any measurable function
h:\mathcal{H}_{1+t}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
, the expectation under
\mathbb{P}_{d^{{(m)}}}
is identified as

\displaystyle E[h(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}}))]=\mathbb{E}\bigg[h(H_{1+t})\prod_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1[A_{i}=d^{{(m)}}(H_{1})]}{\pi_{i}(A_{i}\mid H_{i})}\bigg].

(S6.29)

We will prove Lemma
S6.17
by mathematical induction. We consider the following induction hypothesis: under the setup of Lemma
S6.17
, for each
t\in[T]
, (P.i) (
S6.28
) holds, and

\displaystyle(P.ii)\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{i}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{i}(H_{i}))]}{\pi_{i}(A_{i}\mid H_{i})}\Big(\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}))-\Psi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}))\Big)\bigg]\to_{m}0,

(S6.30)

where the product over empty range is defined to be one.
We will denote
\mathbf{p}_{t}(H_{t};f^{(m)})
by
\mathbf{p}_{t}^{(m)}(H_{t})
throughout the proof of Lemma
S6.17
. First we show that the induction hypotheses (P.i) and (P.ii) hold for
t=1
.

S6.3.2
Case
t=1

Note that

\displaystyle V^{\psi}_{*}-V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))]-\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}^{(m)}(H_{1}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))]

\displaystyle\ +\mathbb{E}\Big[\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{(m)}(H_{1}))\Big]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))],

where (a) follows from Lemma
S6.14
and (b) follows as
Lemma
S6.15
implies

\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\geq\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1})).

Also, the RHS of the above display is non-negative because
\Psi_{1}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\geq\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))
by the definition of
\Psi^{*}
in (
4.2
).
Since
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V_{*}^{\psi}
, it then follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))]\to_{m}

\displaystyle 0,

(S6.31)

implying (
S6.30
). Hence Hypothesis P.ii holds for
t=1
.
Let
\mathcal{B}_{1}=[c_{1},c_{2}]^{k_{1}}
where
c_{1},c_{2}>0
is as in Lemma
S6.12
. Then Lemma
S6.12
implies that
\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(h_{1})\in\mathcal{B}_{1}
for all
h_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}
. Note that
c_{2}
depends on
\mathbb{P}
. Since
\phi_{1}
satisfies Condition
N1
, Lemma
S6.3
applies on
\phi_{1}
with
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(h_{1})
and
\mathfrak{B}=\mathcal{B}_{1}
.
Let
\varrho
be as in Lemma
S6.3
, which depends on
\phi_{1}
and
\mathbb{P}
(via
\mathcal{B}_{1}
). Since
\varrho
is convex with
\text{dom}(\varrho)\supset\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\in\text{dom}(\varrho)
for all
H_{1}
. Thus Jensen’s inequality implies that

\displaystyle\varrho\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\Big[\varrho\Big(\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\Big[\Psi_{1}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\Psi_{1}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\Big],

where (a) follows from Lemma
S6.3
because
\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1})\in\mathcal{B}_{1}
. Lemma
S6.3
states that
\varrho(x)\to 0
implies
x\to 0
for
x\geq 0
. Since
\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\geq\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))
by the definition of
\kappa
in (
S6.3
),

\mathbb{E}[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))]\geq 0.

Therefore, (
S6.31
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\Big]\to_{m}0.

(S6.32)

On one hand, Lemma
S6.13
implies

\mathbb{E}[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))]=V_{*}=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\bigg].

On the other hand,

\begin{split}&\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))=\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1})_{\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}&\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=2}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\bigg]\ \bigg|\ H_{1},A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))\bigg],\end{split}

where (a) follows by (
S6.13
).
Then (
S6.5
) implies

\mathbb{E}[\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))]=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=2}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\frac{1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\bigg],

which, combined with (
S6.32
), implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=2}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\frac{1[A_{1}=d_{1}^{*}(H_{1})]-1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\pi_{1}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}\bigg]\to_{m}0,

(S6.33)

which implies induction hypothesis P.i
holds when
t=1
. If
T=1
, then there is nothing else to prove. Therefore, we will consider the case
T\geq 2
from now on.

S6.3.3
Case
t\in[2:T]

Let
T\geq 2
. Suppose the induction hypotheses P.i and P.ii hold for
t\in[1:T-1]
. We will show that they hold for
t+1
. Let us denote

w_{t}=\prod_{i=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{i}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{i}(H_{i}))]}{\pi_{i}(A_{i}\mid H_{i})},

(S6.34)

implying
w_{1}=1
.
Since
t<T
,
p_{t}
and
p_{t}^{*}
are as in (
S6.1
) and (
S6.2
), respectively.
Using the fact that
w_{t}\geq 0
and equations (
S6.17
) and (
S6.18
) from the proof of Lemma
S6.15
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{t}\Big(\Psi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});\mathbf{p}_{t}^{*}(H_{t}))-\Psi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});\mathbf{p}^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{t}\sum_{i=1}^{k_{t}}\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});i)\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))

\displaystyle\ -\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=i]\bigg]

\displaystyle\ +\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{t}\sum_{i=1}^{k_{t}}\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});i)\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))

\displaystyle\ -\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=i]\bigg],

whose second term is non-negative by Lemma
S6.15
.
Since the
\phi_{t}
’s are non-negative and

\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\geq\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))

for all
t\in[T-1]
, and (
S6.30
) holds for
t
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{t}\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t})))\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))

(S6.35)

\displaystyle\ -\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]\bigg]\to_{m}0.

An application of (
S6.5
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{t}\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t})))\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))

\displaystyle\ -\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t},A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{t}\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}))]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t})))\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))

\displaystyle\ -\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))\mid H_{t}]\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1[A_{i}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{i}(H_{i}))]}{\pi_{i}(A_{i}\mid H_{i})}\bigg)\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t})))

\displaystyle\ \times\Big(\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))\Big)\bigg],

where (a) follows from (
S6.34
). However, the above, combined with (
S6.29
), implies that

\displaystyle E\bigg[\phi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})))\Big)\Big(\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}})))

\displaystyle\ -\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}}));\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}})))\Big)\bigg]\to_{m}0.

While we use
H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})
, we want to remind the readers that the functions
\mathbf{p}_{t}
and
\mathbf{p}_{t}^{*}
do not depend on the distribution of
H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})
, and their definition remains as in as in (
S6.1
) and (

), respectively, which depends only on
\mathbb{P}
.
Lemma
S6.18
below states that the above convergence implies

\displaystyle E\bigg[\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}})))-\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}}));\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}})))\bigg]\to_{m}0,

which combined with (
S6.29
), implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{1+t}\bigg(\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))\bigg)\bigg]\to_{m}0.

Lemma S6.18
.

Consider the setup of Lemma
S6.17
. Let
T\geq 2
and

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}=\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}})))-\Psi_{1+t}(f_{1+t}(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}}));\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}(d^{{(m)}})))

(S6.36)

for some
t\in[T-1]
.
Suppose (
S6.30
) holds and

\displaystyle E\bigg[\phi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})))\Big){\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}\bigg]\to_{m}0.

(S6.37)

Then, under the setup of Lemma
S6.17
,

E[{\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}]\to_{m}0
.

Lemma
S6.18
is proved in Section
S6.3.4
.
Since
t\geq 1
, we have
1+t\geq 2
. Therefore, Lemma
S6.8
implies

\displaystyle\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\Psi_{1+t}(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \chi_{\phi_{1+t}}\Big(\max(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\Big).

(S6.38)

Since
w_{t}
’s are non-negative by (
S6.34
), we therefore obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{1+t}\Big(\Psi_{1+t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\Psi_{1+t}(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \chi_{\phi_{1+t}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{1+t}\Big(\max(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\Big)\bigg].

Thus (
S6.38
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{1+t}\Big(\max(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\Big)\bigg]\to_{m}0.

(S6.39)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{1+t}\Big(\max(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{1+t}\mathbb{E}\Big[\max(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t})_{\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))}\mid H_{1+t}\Big]\bigg].

Now we want to use (
S6.9
) with
H_{1+t}
,
h(H_{1+t})=1
, and

\mathbb{V}=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=2+t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}.

Note that (
S6.13
) implies

\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{V}\mid H_{1+t},A_{1+t}=i]=\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t})_{i}\text{ for all }i\in[k_{1+t}].

Therefore, (
S6.9
) implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t}))-\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(H_{1+t})_{\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))}\bigg|H_{1+t}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]-1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]}{\pi_{1+t}(A_{1+t}\mid H_{1+t})}\mathbb{V}\bigg|H_{1+t}\bigg].

Thus we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{1+t}\Big(\max(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[w_{1+t}\bigg\{\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=2+t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\ \times\frac{\Big(1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]-1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]\Big)}{\pi_{1+t}(A_{1+t}\mid H_{1+t})}\bigg],

If
2+t=T+1
, the product will be one since the range is empty.
Hence, (
S6.39
) implies that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)w_{1+t}\prod_{j=2+t}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\\
&\ \times\frac{\Big(1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]-1[A_{1+t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1+t}(H_{1+t}))]\Big)}{\pi_{1+t}(A_{1+t}\mid H_{1+t})}\bigg]\to_{m}0,\end{split}

which proves P.i for
1+t
.

S6.3.4
The proof of Lemma
S6.18

Proof of Lemma
S6.18
.

First, we will show that the
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}
in (
S6.36
) is non-negative and bounded above. Our
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}
is also non-negative by the definition of
\Psi_{t}^{*}
in (
4.2
). The boundedness follows because the
\phi_{t}
’s are bounded and
\mathbf{p}_{1+t}^{*}(h_{1+t})
is bounded above by a constant
c_{1}
, potentially depending on
\mathbb{P}
, for all
h_{1+t}\in\mathcal{H}_{1+t}
—as shown in Lemma
S6.12
.

It suffices to show that given any subsequence of
\{m\}
, there exists a subsequence along which
E[{\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}]\to_{m}0
. First note that

\phi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})))\Big){\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}\geq 0,

(
S6.37
) implies

\displaystyle\phi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})))\Big){\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}\to_{P}0.

(S6.40)

The convergence in (
S6.30
) implies that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{t-1}\frac{1[A_{i}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{i}(H_{i}))]}{\pi_{i}(A_{i}\mid H_{i})}\Big(\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}))-\Psi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}))\Big)\bigg]\to_{m}0.

Thus (
S6.29
) leads to

E\bigg[\Psi_{t}^{*}\Big(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))\Big)-\Psi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))\Big)\bigg]\to_{m}0.

The random variable inside the above expectation is non-negative by (
4.2
), which implies the above convergence is
L_{1}
-convergence. Since
L_{1}
convergence implies convergence in probability,

\displaystyle\Psi_{t}^{*}\Big(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))\Big)-\Psi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))\Big)\to_{P}0,

(S6.41)

where
P
corresponds to the underlying probability space.
Since convergence in probability a implies almost sure convergence along a subsequence, given any subsequence of
m
, we can find a further subsequence
\{m_{r}\}_{r\geq 1}\subset\{m\}
so that
the convergences in (
S6.40
) and (
S6.41
) are almost sure convergences w.r.t. the underlying probability
P
. The proof will be complete if we can show that
\mathbb{E}[Z^{(m_{r})}]\to_{r}0
. For the sake of notational simplicity, we will denote the subsequence
m_{r}
by
m
. Hence, we need to show that if

\displaystyle\phi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}})))\Big){\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}\to_{a.s.}0

(S6.42)

and

\displaystyle\Psi_{t}^{*}\Big(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))\Big)-\Psi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))\Big)\to_{a.s.}0

(S6.43)

as
m\to\infty
, then
then it holds that
\mathbb{E}[Z^{(m)}]\to_{m}0
. Now we will use Lemma
S6.6
with
\mathbf{x}^{(m)}=f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))
and
\mathbf{p}^{(m)}=\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))
. This lemma applies because Lemma
S6.12
implies
\mathbf{p}_{t}^{*}(h_{t})\in[c_{1},c_{2}]^{k_{t}}
for all
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}_{t}
, which indicates that
\|\mathbf{p}_{t}^{*}(h_{t})\|_{1}
is bounded below by a constant, perhaps depending on
\mathbb{P}
, for all
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}_{t}
. Let
\mathcal{J}_{t}>0
be the fixed constant appearing in Lemma
S6.6
, which depends only on
\phi_{t}
.
Lemma
S6.6
implies

\begin{split}&P\bigg(\liminf_{m\to\infty}\phi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))\Big)>\mathcal{J}_{t}\bigg)\\
\geq&\ P\bigg(\Psi_{t}^{*}\Big(\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))\Big)-\Psi_{t}\Big(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}));\mathbf{p}^{*}_{t}(H_{t}(d^{{(m)}}))\Big)\to_{m}0\bigg),\end{split}

which is one because of (
S6.43
).
Then (
S6.42
), combined with the fact that
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}\geq 0
implies
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}\to_{a.s.}0
as
m\to\infty
.
Since
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}
is a bounded random variable, Fact
S11.5
implies
E[{\mathfrak{Z}}^{{(m)}}]\to_{m}0
, which completes the proof.

∎

S6.3.5
New proof of main lemma commented below

S6.4
Proof of Proposition
1

We show the proof first for the case

C_{\phi_{t}}=1
for each
t\in[T]
.
For any
f\in\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle V^{\psi}_{*}-V^{\psi}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))]-\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1}(f_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}(H_{1}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\Psi_{1}(f_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))]

(S6.44)

\displaystyle\ +\mathbb{E}\Big[\Psi_{1}(f_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\Psi_{1}(f_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}(H_{1}))\Big],

where the first term is non-negative, and by Lemma
S6.16
, the second term is bounded below by

C\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\begin{matrix}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f_{1}(H_{1}))]\\
\times\Big(\prod_{r=2}^{T}1[A_{r}=d_{r}^{*}(H_{r})]-\prod_{r=2}^{T}1[A_{r}=\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))]\Big)\end{matrix}}{\prod_{r=1}^{T}\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\ \Bigg|\ H_{1}\right],

where
C=\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{T}\mathcal{J}_{i}\Big)\min_{2\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}
. Lemma
S6.16
also indicates that the above expression is non-negative.

Lemma
S6.8
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Psi_{1}^{*}(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))-\Psi_{1}(f_{1}(H_{1});\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\Big]\geq\chi_{\phi_{1}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1})-\kappa(f_{1}(H_{1}),\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\Big]\Big)

where
\chi_{\phi_{1}}
is as in Lemma
S6.8
. Then
(
S6.44
) reduces to

\displaystyle V^{\psi}_{*}-V^{\psi}(f)\geq\chi_{\phi_{1}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1})-\kappa(f_{1}(H_{1}),\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\ C\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\times 1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\prod_{r=1}^{T}\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}

\displaystyle\ \times\Big(\prod_{r=2}^{T}1[A_{r}=d_{r}^{*}(H_{r})]-\prod_{r=2}^{T}1[A_{r}=\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))]\Big)\ \bigg|\ H_{1}\bigg].

where
C=\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{T}\mathcal{J}_{i}\Big)\min_{2\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}
. Lemma
S6.13
implies that
\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1})=Q_{1}^{*}(H_{1})
. Thus

\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))=\max(Q_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))=Q_{1}^{*}(H_{1},d_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))

and

\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}),\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(h_{1}))=Q_{1}^{*}(H_{1},\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(h_{1}))).

However,

Q_{1}^{*}(H_{1},i)=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=2}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\ \bigg|\ H_{1},A_{1}=i\bigg]

by Fact
S11.4
. Therefore,

\displaystyle E\Big[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(h_{1}))-\kappa(f^{(m)}_{1}(h_{1}),\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(h_{1}))\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=2}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\ \bigg|\ H_{1},A_{1}=d_{1}^{*}(H_{1})\bigg]

\displaystyle\ -\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=2}^{T}\frac{1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\ \bigg|\ H_{1},A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)\prod_{j=2}^{T}1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\prod_{j=1}^{T}\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\bigg(\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}1[A_{1}=d_{1}^{*}(H_{1})]\\
-1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]\end{array}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ V^{*}-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\frac{\prod_{j=2}^{T}1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\prod_{j=1}^{T}\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}_{1}(H_{1}))]\bigg]

(S6.47)

Now
(
S6.47
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\max(\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1})-\kappa(f_{1}(H_{1}),\mathbf{p}_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ V^{*}-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\frac{\prod_{j=2}^{T}1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\prod_{j=1}^{T}\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f_{1}(H_{1}))]\bigg],

implying

\displaystyle V^{\psi}_{*}-V^{\psi}(f)\geq\chi_{\phi_{1}}\bigg(V^{*}-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\frac{\prod_{j=2}^{T}1[A_{j}=d_{j}^{*}(H_{j})]}{\prod_{j=1}^{T}\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f_{1}(H_{1}))]\bigg]\bigg)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\ C\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{T}Y_{i}\Big)1[A_{1}=\text{pred}(f_{1}(H_{1}))]}{\prod_{r=1}^{T}\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\right.

\displaystyle\ \left.\times\Big(\prod_{r=2}^{T}1[A_{r}=d_{r}^{*}(H_{r})]-\prod_{r=2}^{T}1[A_{r}=\text{pred}(f_{r}(H_{r}))]\Big)\ \bigg|\ H_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ \min(\chi_{\phi_{1}},C)\Big(V^{*}-V(f)\Big].

where the last step follows because both terms in the sum are non-negatives. Note that

\min(\chi_{\phi_{1}},C)\geq\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{T}\min(\mathcal{J}_{i},1)\Big)\min_{1\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}.

However, since
\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})=1
for each
t\in[T]
, it follows that
\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}))\leq 1
for each
t\in[T]
. Therefore,
\mathcal{J}_{t}\leq 1
. Hence,

\min(\chi_{\phi_{1}},C)\geq\Big(\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{J}_{t}\Big)\min_{1\leq i\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{i}}.

Thus we have showed

\displaystyle V^{\psi}_{*}-V^{\psi}(f)\geq\Big(\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{J}_{t}\Big)\min_{1\leq t\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{t}}\Big(V_{*}-V(f)\Big)

(S6.48)

when
C_{\phi_{t}}=1
for each
t\in[T]
.

Now suppose
C_{\phi_{t}}\neq 1
.
Then (
S6.48
) holds if we transform
\phi_{t}\mapsto\phi_{t}/C_{\phi_{t}}
. However,
\mathcal{J}_{t}
a also becomes
\mathcal{J}_{t}/C_{\phi_{t}}
following this transformation. Therefore,
(
S6.48
) implies

\frac{V^{\psi}_{*}-V^{\psi}(f)}{\prod_{t=1}^{T}C_{\phi_{t}}}\geq\bigg(\prod_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{J}_{t}}{C_{\phi_{t}}}\Big)\bigg)\min_{1\leq t\leq T}\chi_{\phi_{t}}\Big(V_{*}-V(f)\Big),

which completes the proof.

S7
Proof of Theorem
4.2

This section is organized as follows. Section
S7.1
contains auxiliary lemmas necessary for proving the necessity of Condition
N1
. The proof of the necessity of Condition
N1
is then given in Section
S7.2
. We establish the necessity of Condition
N2
in Section
S7.3
.

S7.1
Lemmas required for proving Condition
N1

Lemma S7.1
.

Suppose
\mathbb{P}
satisfies Assumption I-IV and
Y_{t}\geq 0
for
t\in[T]
. Let
T\geq 2
. Additionally,
Y_{t}=0
for
t>r
where
r\in[T-1]
is an integer, and

Y_{r}\perp O_{r+1},A_{r+1},\ldots,O_{T},A_{T}\mid H_{r},A_{r}.

Then
Q_{t}^{*}(H_{t},A_{t})=0
for all
t>r
and
Q_{r}^{*}(H_{r},a_{r})=\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid H_{r},A_{r}=a_{r}]
for all
a_{r}\in[k]
. Additionally,

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\bigg]=\prod_{t=1+r}^{T}\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\bigg].

Proof of Lemma
S7.1

Proof of Lemma
S7.1
.

First of all, note that for any
a\in[k_{T}]
,

Q^{*}_{T}(H_{T},a)=\mathbb{E}[Y_{T}\mid H_{T},a]=0.

Similarly, we can show that
if
T-1>r
, for any
a\in[k_{T-1}]
,

\displaystyle Q^{*}_{T-1}(H_{T-1},a)=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\Big[Y_{T-1}+\max_{a_{T}\in[k_{T}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{T}\mid H_{T},a_{T}]\ \bigg|\ H_{t-1},A_{t-1}=a\bigg]=0.

By induction, we can show that
Q^{*}_{t}(h_{t},a_{t})=0
for all
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}_{t}
and
a_{t}\in[k_{t}]
as long as
t>r
.
Therefore, for any
a_{r}\in[k_{r}]
,

Q^{*}_{r}(H_{r},a_{r})=\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}+Q^{*}_{r+1}(H_{r+1},A_{r+1})\mid H_{r},A_{r}=a_{r}]=\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid H_{r},A_{r}=a_{r}],

which proves the first part of the lemma.

To prove the second part of the current lemma, we will show that for any
m\in[r,T-1]
,

\begin{split}&\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\bigg]\\
=&\ \prod_{t=1+m}^{T}\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{m}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg].\end{split}

(S7.1)

To prove (
S7.1
), we will show that the statement holds for
m=T-1
. Then we will show that if (
S7.1
) holds for some
m
, then it holds for
m-1
as well, provided
m-1\geq r
. Then, by induction, it will follow that
m=1+r
satisfies (
S7.1
), which will complete the proof of the current lemma.

To this end, note that

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{T}(f_{T}(H_{T});A_{T})}{\pi_{T}(A_{T}\mid H_{T})}\ \bigg|\ H_{T},A_{T}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{T}(f_{T}(H_{T});\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{T}}}\Psi_{T}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\bigg],

where (a) follows from (
S6.4
) and step (b) uses the non-negativity of the
Y_{t}
’s and the
\phi_{t}
’s (by our assumption).
Note that
\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{T}}}\sum_{i=1}^{k_{T}}\phi_{T}(\mathbf{x};i)=\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})
.
Thus we have shown that

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\bigg]=\Psi^{*}_{T}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg],

which proves (
S7.1
) holds for
m=T-1
.
If
r=T-1
, then there is nothing to prove. Suppose
r\leq T-2
(note that it requires
T>2
for such
r\in\mathbb{N}
to exist).

Now suppose (
S7.1
) holds for some
m\leq T-1
such that
m\geq 1+r
. Then we will show that (
S7.1
) holds for
m-1
as well.
Since
m\geq 1+r
, it holds that
m-1\geq r
and
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r}\subset H_{m}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{m}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{m-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{m}(f_{m}(H_{m});A_{m})}{\pi_{m}(A_{m}\mid H_{m})}\ \bigg|\ H_{m},A_{m}\bigg]\bigg]

Using (
S6.4
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{m}(f_{m}(H_{m});A_{m})}{\pi_{m}(A_{m}\mid H_{m})}\ \bigg|\ H_{m},A_{m}\bigg]=\Psi_{m}(f_{m}(H_{m});\mathbf{1}_{k_{m}}).

Using the non-negativity of the
Y_{t}
’s and the non-negativity of he
\phi_{t}
’s, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{m}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{m-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{m-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{m}}}\Psi_{m}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{1}_{k_{m}})\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{m}}}\Psi_{m}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{1}_{k_{m}})\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{m-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{t\in[r]}Y_{t}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{m-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg].

Since
\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{m}}}\Psi_{m}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{1}_{k_{m}})=\Psi_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{m}})
and (
S7.1
) holds for
m
, the above implies that (
S7.1
) holds for
m-1
as well, thus completing the proof.
∎

Lemma S7.2
.

Let
r\in[T]
. Suppose
\mathbb{P}
is as in the setup of Theorem
4.2
. Moreover,
Y_{t}\geq 0
for
t\in[T]
and
Y_{t}=0
for all
t\neq r
. Further suppose

(H_{r-1},A_{r-1})\perp(A_{r},O_{r},Y_{r})
,
and
Y_{r}\perp(O_{r+1},A_{r+1},\ldots,O_{T},A_{T})\mid(O_{r},A_{r})
. Here
H_{t},A_{t}=\emptyset
if
t<1
or
t>T
. Then

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\bigg]=\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{r}^{*}(Q_{r}^{*}(O_{r}))]\prod_{t=1,t\neq r}^{T}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}),

where

Q_{r}^{*}(O_{r})_{a_{r}}=Q_{r}^{*}(H_{r},a_{r})=\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid O_{r},A_{r}=a_{r}]\quad\text{ for }a_{r}\in[k_{r}].

Proof of Lemma
S7.2
.

Note that
Y_{t}=0
is possible under the setup of Theorem
4.2
.
By Lemma
S7.1
,

Q^{*}_{r}(H_{r},a)=\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid H_{r},A_{r}=a].

Moreover,
Y_{r}\perp(H_{r-1},A_{r-1},Y_{r-1})
where we used the fact that
Y_{r-1}=0
. Therefore,
\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid H_{r},A_{r}]=\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid O_{r},A_{r}]
.
Therefore,

Q^{*}_{r}(H_{r},a)=\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid O_{r},A_{r}=a]\quad\text{ for all }a\in[k_{r}],

which implies
Q_{r}^{*}(O_{r})_{a}=Q_{r}^{*}(H_{r},a)
for
a\in[k_{r}]
.

When
T=1
, the only possible value for
r
is
1
. In that case,

\sup_{f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{1}(f_{1}(H_{1});A_{1})}{\pi_{t}(A_{1}\mid H_{1})}Y_{1}\bigg]=\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1}^{*}(Q_{1}^{*}(H_{1}))]

by
(
S6.7
). Therefore, the proof follows for
T=1
case trivially.
If
T>1
and
r=1
, then from Lemma
S7.1
it follows that

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\bigg]=\prod_{t=2}^{T}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\sup_{f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}Y_{r}\bigg],

which equals
\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{r}^{*}(Q_{r}(H_{r}))]\prod_{t=2}^{T}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})
by
(
S6.7
). Therefore, the proof of the current lemma follows trivially also when
r=1
.

Hence, we consider the case
r>1
. Note that if
r>1
, then
T\geq 2
.
Lemma
S7.1
implies when
r\in[T-1]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\bigg]=\prod_{t=1+r}^{T}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}Y_{r}\bigg].

The product in the above expression is one if
r=T
. It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}Y_{r}\bigg]=\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{*}_{r}(Q^{*}_{r}(O_{r}))]\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}).

(S7.2)

Suppose
V
is any non-negative random variable that is a function of
(H_{r-1},A_{r-1})
.
Using Assumption I and the non-negativity of the
\phi_{t}
’s, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[V\frac{\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}Y_{r}\bigg]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[V\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}Y_{r}\ \bigg|\ H_{r}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[V\sum_{a_{r}\in[k_{r}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid H_{r},A_{r}=a_{r}]\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});a_{r})\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[V\sum_{a_{r}\in[k_{r}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid O_{r},A_{r}=a_{r}]\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});a_{r})\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[V\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{r}}}\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x};Q_{r}^{*}(O_{r}))\bigg],

where (a) follows because
V
is a function of
H_{r-1}
and
A_{r-1}
, (b) follows because the propensity scores are bounded away from zero by Assumption I, (c) follows because
Y_{r}\perp H_{r-1},A_{r-1},Y_{r-1}
(we again used the fact that
Y_{r-1}
, being zero, is independent of
Y_{r}
) and (d) follows using the non-negativity of
V
.
Therefore, we have proved that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[V\frac{\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}Y_{r}\bigg]=\mathbb{E}[V\Psi^{*}_{r}(Q_{r}^{*}(O_{r}))]=\mathbb{E}[V]\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{*}_{r}(Q_{r}^{*}(O_{r}))]

(S7.3)

because
O_{r}\perp(H_{r-1},A_{r-1})
and
V
is a function of
(H_{r-1},A_{r-1})
.
Letting
V=1
in (
S7.3
), we obtain that

\sup_{f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}Y_{r}\bigg]=\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{*}_{r}(Q_{r}^{*}(O_{r}))].

Letting

\displaystyle V=\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})},

we obtain that

\sup_{f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}Y_{r}\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{*}_{r}(Q_{r}^{*}(O_{r}))].

Therefore,

\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}Y_{r}\bigg]=\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{*}_{r}(Q_{r}^{*}(O_{r}))].

The proof will be complete if we can show that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]=\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}).

(S7.4)

For any
j\leq r-1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{j}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{j}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{j}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{j}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{j}(f_{j}(H_{j});A_{j})}{\pi_{j}(A_{j}\mid H_{j})}\ \bigg|\ H_{j},A_{j}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{j}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{j}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{j}(f_{j}(H_{j});\mathbf{1}_{k_{j}})\bigg]

where (a) follows from (
S6.4
).
Since the
\phi_{t}
’s are non-negative, the above equals

\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{j}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{j}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{j}})\bigg]=\Psi_{j}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{j}})\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{j-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{j}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg].

Equation
S7.4
follows by applying (
S6.4
) repeatedly to
j=r-1,\ldots,1
.

∎

S7.2
Proving the necessity of Condition
N1

We will prove by contradiction.
Suppose there exists
r\in[T]
so that
\phi_{r}
does not satisfy Condition
N1
. Hence, there exists
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{r}}_{\geq 0}
that violates (
4.4
) with
\Psi_{r}
.
Suppose
\mathbf{p}
is such that all elements of
\mathbf{p}
are equal. Then the left hand side of (
4.4
) is
\infty
since we defined the supremum of emptyset to be
-\infty
. Therefore, (
4.4
) automatically holds. Therefore, in what follows, we assume that the elements of
\mathbf{p}
are not all equal. Since (
4.4
) fails, there exists
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{k_{r}}
and a sequence
\{\mathbf{x}^{m,r}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{k_{r}}
so that as
m\to\infty
,
\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r};\mathbf{p})\mapsto\Psi_{r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})
but
\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r})}<\max(\mathbf{p})
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
We will show that if the above-mentioned
\mathbf{x}^{m,r}
exists, then we can construct a
\mathbb{P}
and a sequence of class score vectors (these are vector-valued functions)
\{f^{(m)}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\mathcal{F}
so that
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V^{\psi}_{*}
but
V(f^{(m)})\not\to V_{*}
, which will complete the proof. For
t\in[T]
such that
t\neq r
, we define the sequences
\{\mathbf{x}^{m,t}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
so that
\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\to_{m}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})
.

We will define our
f^{(m)}
and
\mathbb{P}
now. We take
f^{(m)}
to be so that
f^{(m)}_{t}\equiv\mathbf{x}^{m,t}
for all
t\in[T]
, i.e.,
f^{(m)}_{t}(h_{t})=\mathbf{x}^{m,t}
for all
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}_{t}
.
We let
\mathbb{P}
be as in Lemma
S7.2
, i.e.,
\mathbb{P}
satisfies Assumptions I-IV,
Y_{t}=0
for all
t\neq r
, and
Y_{r}\geq 0
. Note that
Y_{t}=0
is allowed because under the set up of Theorem
4.2
,
\mathbb{P}
does not need to satisfy Assumption V. Additionally, the independence conditions

(H_{r-1},A_{r-1})\perp(A_{r},O_{r},Y_{r})

and

Y_{r}\perp(O_{r+1},A_{r+1},\ldots,O_{T},A_{T})\mid(O_{r},A_{r})

hold for all
t\in[T]
, where
H_{t}
and
A_{t}
are empty sets if
t<1
or
t>T
. Furthermore, we assume that

\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid H_{r},A_{r}=i]=\mathbf{p}_{i}\quad\text{for }i\in[k].

It is straightforward to verify the existence of such distributions.
From Lemma
S7.2
, it follows that

Q_{r}^{*}(H_{r},A_{r})=\mathbf{p}_{A_{r}}
and

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}Y_{r}\bigg]=\Psi_{r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})\prod_{t\neq r}\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}).

(S7.5)

First, we will show that
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V^{\psi}_{*}
. Next, we will show that
V(f^{(m)})\not\to_{m}V_{*}
, which will contradict the Fisher consistency of
\psi
, hence completing the proof by contradiction.

Showing
\mathbb{V}^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V^{\psi}_{*}

It suffices to show that for each
m\in[\mathbb{N}]
,

\displaystyle V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})=\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r};\mathbf{p})\prod_{t\neq r,t=1}^{T}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})

(S7.6)

because the above converges to
\Psi_{r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})\prod_{t\neq r,t=1}^{T}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})
due to
\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r};\mathbf{p})\to_{m}\Psi_{r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})
and
\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\to_{m}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})
for
t\neq r
. However, (
S7.5
) implies

V^{\psi}_{*}=\Psi_{r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})\prod_{t\neq r,t=1}^{T}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}),

which would complete the proof of
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V^{\psi}_{*}
.

To show (
S7.6
), we will first show that for any
r\in[T]
,

\begin{split}V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})=&\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]\\
=&\ \prod_{t=1+r}^{T}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg].\end{split}

(S7.7)

If
r=T
, (
S7.7
) holds trivially because the product in (
S7.7
) becomes one since the range of the product becomes empty. For
r\in[T-1]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\Psi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{T}(H_{T});A_{T})}{\pi_{T}(A_{T}\mid H_{T})}\ \bigg|\ H_{T},A_{T}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{T}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \Psi_{T}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg],

where (a) follows from (
S6.4
). Proceeding similarly and applying (
S6.4
) repeatedly, we can show (
S7.7
) holds.
To finish the proof of (
S7.6
), now observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\frac{\phi_{r}(f^{(m)}_{r}(H_{r});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{r}(f^{(m)}_{r}(H_{r});Q^{*}_{r}(O_{r}))\bigg]\bigg],

where (a) follows from (
S6.6
) with

Q^{*}_{r}(O_{r})_{j}=\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid H_{r},A_{r}=j]=\mathbf{p}_{j}

by our definition of
\mathbb{P}
.
Since
f^{(m)}_{r}=\mathbf{x}^{m,r}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]=\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r};\mathbf{p})\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg].

Using (
S6.4
) and the fact that
f^{(m)}(H_{t})=\mathbf{x}^{m,t}
is non-stochastic, we can show that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f^{(m)}_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]=\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}).

Therefore, (
S7.6
) follows, completing the proof of
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V^{\psi}_{*}
.

Showing
\limsup_{m\to\infty}V(f^{(m)})<V_{*}

It remains to prove that
V(f^{(m)})\not\to_{m}V_{*}
. First, we will prove that
V(f^{(m)})=\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r})}
. To see this, note that

\displaystyle V(f^{(m)})=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{t}))]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{t}))]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1[A_{T}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{T}))]}{\pi_{T}(A_{T}\mid H_{T})}\ \bigg|\ H_{T}\bigg]\bigg].

Letting the
\phi
in (
S6.4
) be
\phi_{\text{dis}}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1[A_{T}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{T}))]}{\pi_{T}(A_{T}\mid H_{T})}\bigg]=1.

Thus

V(f^{(m)})=\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{t}))]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg],

which rewrites as

\displaystyle V(f^{(m)})=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{t}))]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\frac{1[A_{r}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{r}))]}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\ \bigg|\ H_{r}\bigg]\bigg]

where the product term is one if
r=1
. Letting the
\phi
in (
S6.6
) be
\phi_{\text{dis}}
, we obtain that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{r}\frac{1[A_{r}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{r}))]}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\ \bigg|\ H_{r}\bigg]=\sum_{a\in[k_{r}]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{r}\mid H_{r},a]1[a=\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r})],\end{split}

(S7.8)

which equals
\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r})}
.
Thus

V(f^{(m)})=\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{t}))]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg].

Applying (
S6.4
) repeatedly with
\phi=\phi_{\text{dis}}
, we can show that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{1[A_{t}=\text{pred}(f^{(m)}(H_{t}))]}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]=1.

Hence, we showed that
V(f^{(m)})=\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r})}
.
It remains to show that

\limsup_{m\to\infty}\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r})}<V_{*}
.

By our assumption, for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{(m)})}<\max(\mathbf{p})
. Therefore,
\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{(m)})\notin\argmax(\mathbf{p})
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
\mathbf{p}
has a finite length, this implies
\limsup_{m\to\infty}\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x}^{(m)})}<\max(\mathbf{p})
. Therefore,
\limsup_{m\to\infty}V(f^{(m)})<\max(\mathbf{p})
. Thus, it suffices to show that
V_{*}\geq\max(\mathbf{p})
.
To this end, note that, for any
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{r}}
, setting
f^{(m)}_{r}\equiv\mathbf{x}
,
f^{(m)}_{t}\equiv\mathbf{x}^{m,t}
for
t\neq r
,
f^{(m)}=(f^{(m)}_{1},\ldots,f^{(m)}_{r},\ldots,f^{(m)}_{T})
, and working as before, we can show that

V(f^{(m)})\to_{m}\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})}.

If we take
\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{p}
, then
V(f^{(m)})\to_{m}\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{p})}=\max(\mathbf{p})
. Thus
V_{*}\geq\max(\mathbf{p})
, which completes the proof.

S7.3
Proving the necessity of Condition
N2

We only need to consider the
T\geq 2
case in this proof.
Since we have already proved that
\phi_{t}
’s satisfy Condition
N1
for
t\in[T]
, we will use this fact in the proof. In particular,
\phi_{t}
’s are bounded and
\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{p})\in(0,\infty)
for all
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{k+t}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathbf{p}\neq\mathbf{0}_{k_{t}}
by Lemma
S6.1
and
\Psi_{t}^{*}
is continuous by Lemma
S6.2
. Moreover, Fact
S6.1
will also apply.

We will prove the necessity of
Condition
N2
in 3 steps.
To this end, let
r\in[1:T-1]
.

Step 1.

We will show that for any
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{k_{1+r}}
,
\max(\mathbf{p})>\max(\mathbf{q})
implies
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})\geq\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{q})
.

Step 2.

Using the result from Step 1, we will show that for any
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{k_{1+r}}
,
\max(\mathbf{p})=\max(\mathbf{q})
implies
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})=\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{q})
. This will imply that there exists a function
h:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
so that
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})=h_{1+r}(\max(\mathbf{p}))
for all
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1+r}}_{\geq 0}
.

Step 3.

We will show that
h_{1+r}(x)=\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{1+r}})x
for all
x>0
.
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{1+r}})<\infty
by Lemma
S6.1
.

Step 3 implies
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})=\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{1+r}})\max(\mathbf{p})
for all
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{k_{1+r}}
. Since
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{1+r}})
is positive for each
r\in[T-1]
by Lemma
S6.1
,
\phi_{1+r}
satisfies Condition
N2
. Since
r
can be any number in
[1:T-1]
, it follows that
\phi_{t}
satisfies Condition
N2
if
t\in[2:T]
, thus completing the proof.

S7.3.1
Step 1: Showing
\max(\mathbf{p})>\max(\mathbf{q})
implies
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})\geq\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{q})
for any
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1+r}}_{\geq 0}
and any
r\in[1:T-1]

Note that if
\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{0}_{k_{1+r}}
, then
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{q})=0
. Since the
\phi(\cdot;i)
’s are non-negative,
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})\geq\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{q})
automatically holds. Hence, it suffices to consider the case where
\mathbf{q}\neq\mathbf{0}_{k_{1+r}}
so that
\max(\mathbf{q})>0
. In this case, note that we also have
\mathbf{p}\neq\mathbf{0}_{k_{1+r}}
.
Let
r\in[1:T-1]
. We will prove Step 1 in some substeps.

•

Step 1a:
We will define a simpler class of distributions
\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
, depending on
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{q}
, that satisfy Assumptions I-IV and
Y_{t}\geq 0
for all
t\in[T]
.

•

Step 1b:
We will show that when
\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
, for any collection of non-negative surrogates
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{T}
, we have simpler expressions of
V^{\psi}_{*}
and
V^{\psi}(f)
for some classes of
f
.

•

Step 1c:
Using the form of
V^{\psi}_{*}
, we will derive the form of
V_{*}
by letting
\phi
to be the 0-1 loss.

•

Step 1d:
We will define a sequence of class-score functions
\{f^{(m)}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\mathcal{F}
so that
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V^{\psi}_{*}
.

•

Step 1d:
We calculate
V(f^{(m)})
for the aforementioned sequence of score functions.

•

Step 1e:
Fisher consistency implies
V(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V_{*}
, which leads to the desired inequality
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p})\geq\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{q})
.

Step 1a. Defining the small class
\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}

Suppose
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{q}
are fixed vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{k_{1+r}}_{\geq 0}
such that
\max(\mathbf{p})>\max(\mathbf{q})
.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
be the class of all
\mathbb{P}
’s that satisfy Assumption I-IV and
Y_{t}\geq 0
for all
t\in[T]
in addition to the followings:

(a)

Y_{i}=0
unless
i=1+r
.

(b)

H_{r-1},A_{r-1}\perp(A_{r},O_{r},A_{1+r},O_{1+r},Y_{1+r})
for all
t\in[2:T-1]
. If
r=1
, we take
H_{0}=\emptyset
and
A_{0}=0
. Hence, the above statement is vacuously true for
r=1
.

(c)

O_{r}=O_{1+r}=\emptyset
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y_{1+r}\mid H_{1+r},A_{1+r}]=\mathbb{E}[Y_{1+r}\mid A_{r},A_{1+r}].

Also, for
i\in[k_{r}]
and
j\in[k_{1+r}]
, we let
\mathbb{E}[Y_{1+r}\mid A_{r}=i,A_{1+r}=j]=\mathbf{p}^{(i)}_{j}
where

\displaystyle\mathbf{p}^{(i)}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{p}&\text{ if }i=1\\
\mathbf{q}&\text{ if }i\in[2:k_{r}].\end{cases}

(S7.9)

(d)

Y_{1+r}\perp O_{r+2},A_{2+r},\ldots\mid H_{r}
. We take
O_{T+1}=\emptyset
and
A_{T+1}=0
.

Note that
\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
has resembelence with a 2-stage DTR. The outcome
Y_{r}
does not depend on any variable other than
A_{r}
and
A_{1+r}
. Thus the stages
r
and
1+r
are the only stages that has connection to the outcome. The
T
stage DTR is formed from this 2-stage DTR by padding
r-1
stages before and
T-1-r
after these two stages. The proof builds on the idea that, since the
\phi_{t}
’s are Fisher consistent,
(\phi_{r},\phi_{1+r})
are Fisher consistent for the two-stage DTR embedded in the
T
stage DTR.

Step 1b. Properties under
\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}

Distributions in
\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
have some interesting properties.
First we will show that
V^{\psi}_{*}
has some closed form expression under
\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
for non-negative surrogates.
Let us define
\nu\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{r}}_{\geq 0}
so that

\displaystyle\nu_{i}=\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{(i)})\text{ for each }i\in[k_{r}],

(S7.10)

where the
\mathbf{p}^{(i)}
’s are as defined in (
S7.9
). Here the
\nu_{i}
’s are non-negative by Lemma
S6.1
because
\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{(i)})>0
since
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}\neq\mathbf{0}_{k_{1+r}}
.

Lemma S7.3
.

Suppose
\psi
is as in (
4.1
), where the losses
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{T}
are bounded and non-negative. Let
r\in[1:T-1]
. Then for any
\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
, it follows that

V^{\psi}_{*}=\Psi_{r}^{*}(\nu)\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\prod_{t=2+r}^{T}\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}),

where the products are one if the range is empty and
\nu
is as defined in (
S7.10
)

Proof of Lemma
S7.3
.

First, we want to show

\displaystyle V^{\psi}_{*}=\prod_{t=2+r}^{T}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{1+r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{1+r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg],

(S7.11)

where the products in the integrand are
1
if the range is empty. The equation in (
S7.11
) trivially holds if
r=T-1
. Let us consider the case
r<T-1
. Then

\displaystyle V^{\psi}_{*}=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{T}(f_{T}(H_{T});A_{T})}{\pi_{T}(A_{T}\mid H_{T})}\ \bigg|\ H_{T}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{T}(f_{T}(H_{T});\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{T}}}\Psi_{T}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{T-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

where step (b) uses the non-negativity of
Y_{1+r}
and the
\phi_{t}
’s
and step (a) follows from (
S6.4
). Proceeding as above, we can show (
S7.11
) holds. Observe that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{1+r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{1+r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{1+r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\frac{\phi_{1+r}(f_{1+r}(H_{1+r});A_{1+r})}{\pi_{1+r}(A_{1+r}\mid H_{1+r})}\ \bigg|\ H_{1+r}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{1+r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{1+r}(f_{1+r}(H_{1+r});\mathbf{p}_{2}(H_{1+r}))\bigg]

by (
S6.6
), where

\mathbf{p}_{2}(H_{1+r})_{j}=\mathbb{E}[Y_{1+r}\mid H_{1+r},A_{1+r}=j].

However, for our
\mathbb{P}
,

\mathbb{E}[Y_{1+r}\mid H_{1+r},A_{1+r}=j]=\mathbb{E}[Y_{1+r}\mid A_{r},A_{1+r}=j]=\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})}_{j}\text{ for all }j\in[k_{1+r}]

Therefore,
\mathbf{p}_{2}(H_{1+r})=\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})}
.
Hence, we have shown that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\frac{\phi_{1+r}(f_{1+r}(H_{1+r});A_{1+r})}{\pi_{1+r}(A_{1+r}\mid H_{1+r})}\ \bigg|\ H_{1+r}\bigg]=\Psi_{1+r}(f_{1+r}(H_{1+r});\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})}),

(S7.12)

and, in particular,

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{1+r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{1+r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{1+r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{1+r}(f_{1+r}(H_{1+r});\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1+r}}}\Psi_{1+r}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\ \bigg|\ H_{r}\bigg]\bigg]

where the product term is one if
r=1
.
Another application of (
S6.6
) yields that
the above equals

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});\nu)\bigg],

where

\nu_{j}=\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\mid H_{r},A_{r}=j]=\Psi_{1+r}^{*}(\mathbf{p}^{(j)})\text{ for all }j\in[k_{r}],

i.e.,
\nu
is non-stochastic and as defined in (
S7.10
).
Using the fact that the
\phi_{t}
’s are non-negatve, it then follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});\nu)\bigg]=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{r}}}\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x};\nu)\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi^{*}_{r}(\nu)\bigg].

Thus we have shown that

\displaystyle\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{1+r}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{1+r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]=\Psi_{r}^{*}(\nu)\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg].

The proof follows by combining the above with (
S7.11
), and noting that, for
r-1\geq 1
, (
S7.4
) implies that

\sup_{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r-1}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]=\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}).

∎

For simpler forms of
f
’s, we can find their surrogate value function
V^{\psi}(f)
explicitly under
\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
.

Lemma S7.4
.

Suppose
\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
and
\psi
is as in (
4.1
), where
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{T}
are bounded and non-negative. Consider
r\in[1,T-1]
. For
t\notin\{1+r\}
, we let
f_{t}(h_{t})=\mathbf{x}^{(t)}
for all
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}_{t}
where
\mathbf{x}^{(t)}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
are fixed vectors. For
H_{1+r}=(H_{r},A_{r},Y_{r})
, we let
f_{1+r}(H_{1+r})=\mathcal{M}^{(A_{r})}
where, for each
i\in[k_{r}]
,
\mathcal{M}^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{1+r}}
is a non-stochastic vector.
Let us define

\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{r}}
is defined by

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}=\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{(i)};\mathbf{p}^{(i)})\text{ for all }i\in[k_{r}].

(S7.13)

Then

\displaystyle V^{\psi}(f)=\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x}^{(r)};\gamma)\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{(t)};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\prod_{t=r+2}^{T}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{(t)};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}),

where
\gamma
is as defined in (
S7.13
), the
\mathbf{p}^{(i)}
’s are as in (
S7.9
), and the products are one if the ranges are empty.

Proof of Lemma
S7.4
.

Our first step is to show that

\displaystyle V^{\psi}(f)=\bigg(\prod_{t=2+r}^{T}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{(t)};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\bigg)\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{1+r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg],

(S7.14)

where the product is one if the range of the product is empty.
The above follows trivially if
r=T-1
. Suppose
T>1+r
. Then

\displaystyle V^{\psi}(f)=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\phi_{T}(f_{T}(H_{T});A_{T})}{\pi_{T}(A_{T}\mid H_{T})}\ \bigg|\ H_{T}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\Psi_{T}(f_{T}(H_{T});\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \Psi_{T}(\mathbf{x}^{(T)};\mathbf{1}_{k_{T}})\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

where (a) follows from (
S6.4
).
Proceeding as above, and applying (
S6.4
) repeatedly, (
S7.14
) can be proved.
Using (
S7.12
), we can show that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{1+r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Psi_{1+r}(f_{1+r}(H_{1+r});\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg].

However, according to our definition,

f_{1+r}(H_{1+r})=\mathcal{M}^{(A_{r})},

where
A_{r}\in H_{1+r}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y_{1+r}\prod_{t=1}^{1+r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{(A_{r})};\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\prod_{t=1}^{r}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{(A_{r})};\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\frac{\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{t});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\ \bigg|\ H_{r}\bigg]\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg],

where the product over the range
\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}
is one if
r-1=0
. Another application of (
S6.6
) yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{(A_{r})};\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\frac{\phi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\ \bigg|\ H_{r}\bigg]=\Psi_{r}(f_{r}(H_{r});\gamma),

where

\gamma_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{(A_{r})};\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\mid H_{r},A_{r}=i]=\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{(i)};\mathbf{p}^{(i)}).

Since
f_{r}(H_{r})=\mathbf{x}^{(r)}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{(A_{r})};\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\ \bigg|\ H_{r}\bigg]=\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x}^{(r)};\gamma).

Therefore,

\displaystyle E\bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{(A_{r})};\mathbf{p}^{(A_{r})})\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{r}(H_{r});A_{r})}{\pi_{r}(A_{r}\mid H_{r})}\ \bigg|\ H_{r}\bigg]\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x}^{(r)};\gamma)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg],

where, if
r-1\geq 1
, using (
S6.4
) repeatedly, one can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\frac{\phi_{t}(f_{t}(H_{t});A_{t})}{\pi_{t}(A_{t}\mid H_{t})}\bigg]=\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{(t)};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}).

Therefore, the proof follows.
∎

Step 1c. Calculating
V_{*}

Lemma
S7.3
implies that, for any non-negative
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{T}
,

V^{\psi}_{*}=\Psi_{r}^{*}(\nu)\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\prod_{t=2+r}^{T}\Psi^{*}_{t}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})

where

\nu
is as in (
S7.10
).
Suppose for each
t\in[T]
,
\phi_{t}(\mathbf{x};i)=1[\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})=i]
where
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
.
Then
\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})=1
. Therefore,
\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})=1
. For any
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{k_{t}}
in general,
\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p})=\mathbf{p}_{\text{pred}(\mathbf{x})}
. Therefore,
\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{p})=\max(\mathbf{p})
. Hence, (
S7.10
) implies
\nu_{i}=\Psi^{*}_{1+r}(\mathbf{p}^{(i)})=\max(\mathbf{p}^{(i)})
. Therefore,
V_{*}=\max(\nu)=\max_{i\in[k_{r}]}\max(\mathbf{p}^{(i)})
.

Step 1d. Defining the sequences

Now we will define a sequence of class scores
\{f^{(m)}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\mathcal{F}
. To this end, we define some sequences of vectors first. The following fact will be used while defining these sequences: since
\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{p})=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x};\mathbf{p})
for each
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
, there exists a sequence
\{\mathbf{x}^{(m)}\}_{t\geq 1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
so that
\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{(m)};\mathbf{p})\to_{m}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{p})
for each
\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}_{\geq 0}
and
t\in[T]
.

For each
i\in[k_{r}]
, let
\{\mathcal{M}^{m,i}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{k_{1+r}}
be a sequence that satisfies

\displaystyle\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{m,i};\mathbf{p}^{(i)})\to_{m}\Psi^{*}_{1+r}(\mathbf{p}^{(i)}),

(S7.15)

where
\mathbf{p}^{(i)}
is as in (
S7.9
) for each
i\in[k_{r}]
.
We let
\{\mathbf{x}^{m,r}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{k_{r}}
be a sequence of vectors that satisfy

\displaystyle\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r};\nu)\to_{m}\Psi_{r}^{*}(\nu),

(S7.16)

where
\nu
as in (
S7.10
).
For each
t\notin\{r,1+r\}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
, define the sequences
\{\mathbf{x}^{m,t}\}_{m\geq 1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{k_{t}}
such that
\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\to_{m}\Psi_{t}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})
.

Now we are ready to define
f^{(m)}
. For
t\notin\{r,1+r\}
, we let
f_{t}^{(m)}\equiv\mathbf{x}^{m,t}
, i.e.,
f_{t}^{(m)}(h_{t})=\mathbf{x}^{m,t}
for all
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}_{t}
. When
t=r
, we let
f^{(m)}_{r}\equiv\mathbf{x}^{m,r}
. Thus
f^{(m)}_{t}
is constant vector for all
t\neq 1+r
. However, we will consider a non-constant
f^{(m)}_{1+r}
. Note that
H_{1+r}=(H_{r},A_{r})
for
\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\text{small}}
because
Y_{r}=0
and
O_{r}=O_{1+r}=\emptyset
. Therefore,
f_{1+r}
is a function from
\mathcal{H}_{r}\times[k_{r}]
to
\mathbb{R}
. For
i\in[k_{r}]
, we let
f_{1+r}^{(m)}(H_{1+r})=f_{1+r}^{(m)}(H_{r},i)=\mathcal{M}^{m,i}
.

Step 1e. Showing
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})\to_{m}V^{\psi}_{*}

Our surrogates are non-negative by assumption and they are bounded by Lemma
S6.1
. Therefore,
Lemmas
S7.3
and
S7.4
apply.
V^{\psi}_{*}
is given by Lemma
S7.3
.
V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})
is given by Lemma
S7.4
, which yields that

\displaystyle V^{\psi}(f^{(m)})=\Psi_{r}(\mathbf{x}^{m,r};\gamma^{(m)})\prod_{t=1}^{r-1}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}})\prod_{t=r+2}^{T}\Psi_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{m,t};\mathbf{1}_{k_{t}}),

(S7.17)

where

\gamma^{(m)}=\Psi_{1+r}(\mathcal{M}^{m,i};\mathbf{p}^{(i)})\text{ for all }i\in[k_{r}],
